---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:

---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

result19. Command output
# Agent Brief: Green Hydrogen Hub Resilience Planning

You are helping a regional clean-energy authority prepare a one-day operating
and resilience plan for a network of green hydrogen production hubs. The
network serves transit depots, critical backup-power customers, port drayage,
an industrial pilot, and airport ground equipment. Renewable power, demand,
water permits, module derates, and road capacity all vary across the supplied
stress scenarios.

# Green Hydrogen Hub Resilience Planning

## Planning Goal

Prepare a risk-aware plan for a regional green hydrogen hub over a 12-hour
operating day. The authority must decide a common infrastructure posture before
knowing which stress scenario occurs. After the scenario is realized, the plan
may adapt hourly production, renewable and grid energy use, storage, and tube
trailer deliveries.

The goal is to minimize daily infrastructure cost plus expected operating cost
and a tail-risk surcharge for expensive scenarios. Operating costs include
electrolyzer operation, grid energy, water, module starts, high-purity
polishing, trailer trips, emissions, venting, and unmet hydrogen demand.

## Operational Rules

Infrastructure decisions are shared across all scenarios. A site can operate
only within its selected electrolyzer-module count, tank-module count, and
polishing decision. Tank modules increase usable storage above the listed base
storage. High-purity customers can be served only by hydrogen that was polished
at a site with polishing capability.

Every scenario policy must account for each hour of production-site operation.
Online modules are integer counts. If modules are online, production must be at
least the site's minimum-load fraction and no more than the scenario-adjusted
module capacity. Module starts are limited over the day. Production consumes
energy and water. Renewable energy is limited by the scenario-adjusted
forecast; grid energy is limited by the scenario site-hour cap and counts
toward the scenario emissions cap.

Hydrogen storage is stateful at every site. Ending inventory equals prior
inventory plus production minus outbound deliveries and venting. Inventories
must stay within the selected storage capacity, and final inventory must meet
the site requirement. The whole network must also maintain a system reserve at
the end of every hour.

Deliveries move along listed site-customer lanes. Each lane has a tube-trailer
payload, trip cost, driver-hours requirement, normal hourly trip cap, and
hazmat delivery window. Scenario-hour rows may close or tighten a lane. A
delivery row cannot exceed the trip capacity implied by its submitted trip
count, and scenario-hour driver hours cannot exceed the scenario cap.

Every customer-hour demand must be either delivered or listed as shortfall.
Scenario-level service floors apply by customer. Critical customers have much
tighter hourly shortfall caps, so delaying them across several hours is risky
even if total daily service looks acceptable.

This task is designed for a short solve budget. A strong approach may combine
an infrastructure master problem, scenario subproblems, warm-started MIPs, and
repair heuristics for truck trips and inventories rather than relying on one
large cold-start model.

## Data Dictionary

`data/config.json`

- global conversion factors, reserve target, risk settings, penalties, and
  numerical tolerance.

`data/sites.csv`

- production sites, electrolyzer module limits, minimum load, variable costs,
  tank options, initial/final inventories, water permits, startup limits, and
  polishing costs.

`data/customers.csv`

- customer segments, purity requirement, service floor, hourly shortfall cap,
  and shortage penalty.

`data/lanes.csv`

- eligible tube-trailer lanes from production sites to customers, payloads,
  hourly trip caps, driver hours, trip cost, and hazmat delivery windows.

`data/scenarios.csv`

- scenario probability, narrative label, grid-price multiplier, emissions cap,
  and driver-hour budget.

`data/hourly_forecast.csv`

- site-hour renewable availability, grid price, and grid emissions intensity
  before scenario multipliers.

`data/base_customer_hourly_demand.csv`

- baseline customer demand profile used to interpret scenario demand shifts.

`data/scenario_site_hourly.csv`

- scenario-hour renewable, module-capacity, water-permit, and grid-capacity
  multipliers for each production site.

`data/scenario_customer_hourly_demand.csv`

- realized customer-hour hydrogen demand in each scenario.

`data/scenario_lane_hourly.csv`

- scenario-hour lane trip caps, travel multipliers, and closure flags.

## Submission Format

Create `submissions/solution.json`. The top-level `solution.json` object must contain:

```json
{
  "infrastructure": [],
  "operations": [],
  "deliveries": [],
  "shortfalls": []
}
```

Use one `infrastructure` row for every production site. Use one `operations`
row for every scenario, hour, and site. Use one `shortfalls` row for every
scenario, hour, and customer. `deliveries` may be sparse, but every delivery
row must reference a known lane and include both delivered kilograms and truck
trip count.

`infrastructure` rows:

```json
{
  "site_id": "PORT_SOLAR",
  "active": true,
  "electrolyzer_modules": 4,
  "tank_modules": 3,
  "polishing_active": true
}
```

`operations` rows:

```json
{
  "scenario_id": "BASE",
  "hour": 0,
  "site_id": "PORT_SOLAR",
  "online_modules": 1,
  "startup_modules": 1,
  "production_kg": 30.0,
  "renewable_mwh": 22.0,
  "grid_mwh": 0.0,
  "storage_end_kg": 20.0,
  "vent_kg": 0.0,
  "water_m3": 3.45,
  "polishing_kg": 20.0
}
```

`deliveries` rows:

```json
{
  "scenario_id": "BASE",
  "hour": 0,
  "lane_id": "PORT_SOLAR__PORT_DRAYAGE",
  "kg": 21.0,
  "trips": 1
}
```

`shortfalls` rows:

```json
{
  "scenario_id": "BASE",
  "hour": 1,
  "customer_id": "BUS_DEPOT",
  "kg": 1.0
}
```

Use nonnegative numeric values. Hours are `0` through `11`. Integer fields must
be submitted as whole numbers.

## Required Output

Create these artifacts under `submissions/`:

- `model.md`: a plain-language mathematical model or algorithm description.
- `solve.py`: the script used to create the submitted policy.
- `solution.json`: the infrastructure and scenario policy.
- `solve_log.md`: commands run, solver status, objective or score if known, and
  validation notes.

/bin/bash -lc "python - <<'PY'
import csv, json, math
from collections import defaultdict, Counter
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path('/app')
TOL = 1e-4

def read_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))

def num(x):
    return float(x)

def is_intlike(x):
    return abs(x - round(x)) <= 1e-9

config = json.load(open(ROOT/'data/config.json'))
TOL = config['numerical_tolerance']
sites = {r['site_id']:{
    'max_modules': int(r['max_modules']),
    'module_capacity_kgph': num(r['module_capacity_kgph']),
    'min_load_fraction': num(r['min_load_fraction']),
    'module_daily_cost': num(r['module_daily_cost']),
    'variable_cost_per_kg': num(r['variable_cost_per_kg']),
    'tank_base_kg': num(r['tank_base_kg']),
    'tank_module_capacity_kg': num(r['tank_module_capacity_kg']),
    'max_tank_modules': int(r['max_tank_modules']),
    'tank_daily_cost': num(r['tank_daily_cost']),
    'initial_inventory_kg': num(r['initial_inventory_kg']),
    'final_min_inventory_kg': num(r['final_min_inventory_kg']),
    'water_limit_m3_per_day': num(r['water_limit_m3_per_day']),
    'startup_limit': int(r['startup_limit']),
    'polishing_cost_per_kg': num(r['polishing_cost_per_kg']),
    'polishing_daily_cost': num(r['polishing_daily_cost']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/sites.csv')}
customers = {r['customer_id']:{
    'purity_required_pct': num(r['purity_required_pct']),
    'service_floor_fraction': num(r['service_floor_fraction']),
    'max_hourly_shortfall_fraction': num(r['max_hourly_shortfall_fraction']),
    'shortfall_cost_per_kg': num(r['shortfall_cost_per_kg']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/customers.csv')}
lanes = {r['lane_id']:{
    'origin_site': r['origin_site'],
    'customer_id': r['customer_id'],
    'kg_per_trip': num(r['kg_per_trip']),
    'base_max_trips_per_hour': int(r['base_max_trips_per_hour']),
    'driver_hours_per_trip': num(r['driver_hours_per_trip']),
    'cost_per_trip': num(r['cost_per_trip']),
    'hazmat_window_start': int(r['hazmat_window_start']),
    'hazmat_window_end': int(r['hazmat_window_end']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/lanes.csv')}
scenarios = {r['scenario_id']:{
    'probability': num(r['probability']),
    'grid_price_multiplier': num(r['grid_price_multiplier']),
    'emission_cap_kg': num(r['emission_cap_kg']),
    'driver_hours_cap': num(r['driver_hours_cap']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/scenarios.csv')}
forecast = {(int(r['hour']), r['site_id']):{
    'renewable_mwh': num(r['renewable_mwh']),
    'grid_price_per_mwh': num(r['grid_price_per_mwh']),
    'grid_emission_kg_per_mwh': num(r['grid_emission_kg_per_mwh']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/hourly_forecast.csv')}
scenario_site = {(r['scenario_id'], int(r['hour']), r['site_id']):{
    'renewable_multiplier': num(r['renewable_multiplier']),
    'module_capacity_multiplier': num(r['module_capacity_multiplier']),
    'water_limit_multiplier': num(r['water_limit_multiplier']),
    'grid_cap_mwh': num(r['grid_cap_mwh']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/scenario_site_hourly.csv')}
demand = {(r['scenario_id'], int(r['hour']), r['customer_id']): num(r['demand_kg']) for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/scenario_customer_hourly_demand.csv')}
scenario_lane = {(r['scenario_id'], int(r['hour']), r['lane_id']):{
    'max_trips': num(r['max_trips']),
    'travel_time_multiplier': num(r['travel_time_multiplier']),
    'closed': int(r['closed']),
} for r in read_csv(ROOT/'data/scenario_lane_hourly.csv')}
sol = json.load(open(ROOT/'submissions/solution.json'))

print('scenario_site water multipliers unique sample:', sorted({v['water_limit_multiplier'] for v in scenario_site.values()})[:20], 'count', len({v['water_limit_multiplier'] for v in scenario_site.values()}))
print('module capacity multipliers:', sorted({v['module_capacity_multiplier'] for v in scenario_site.values()}))
print('travel mult values:', sorted({v['travel_time_multiplier'] for v in scenario_lane.values()}))
print('lane max trips values sample:', sorted({v['max_trips'] for v in scenario_lane.values()})[:20])

# coverage and duplicates
families = defaultdict(list)

def add(fam, key, rule, obs, req, repair):
    families[fam].append((key, rule, obs, req, repair))

# infrastructure checks
infra = sol['infrastructure']
seen_sites = Counter()
for i,row in enumerate(infra):
    key=f'infrastructure[{i}]/{row.get(\"site_id\")}'
    for fld in ['site_id','active','electrolyzer_modules','tank_modules','polishing_active']:
        if fld not in row or row[fld] is None:
            add('schema', key, f'missing/non-null field {fld}', row.get(fld), 'present and non-null', 'populate the required field')
    sid=row.get('site_id')
    seen_sites[sid]+=1
    if sid not in sites:
        add('identifier', key, 'site_id must reference sites.csv', sid, 'known site_id', 'use a valid site_id')
        continue
    for fld in ['electrolyzer_modules','tank_modules']:
        if not isinstance(row.get(fld),(int,float)) or not is_intlike(float(row[fld])):
            add('schema', key, f'{fld} must be a whole number', row.get(fld), 'integer', 'round to a valid integer consistent with the plan')
    em=int(round(float(row['electrolyzer_modules']))) if isinstance(row.get('electrolyzer_modules'),(int,float)) else None
    tm=int(round(float(row['tank_modules']))) if isinstance(row.get('tank_modules'),(int,float)) else None
    if em is not None and (em < 0 or em > sites[sid]['max_modules']):
        add('capacity', key, 'selected electrolyzer modules within site max', em, f'0..{sites[sid][\"max_modules\"]}', 'reduce modules or choose a valid site selection')
    if tm is not None and (tm < 0 or tm > sites[sid]['max_tank_modules']):
        add('capacity', key, 'selected tank modules within site max', tm, f'0..{sites[sid][\"max_tank_modules\"]}', 'reduce tank modules to the site limit')
    if row.get('active') is False and ((em or 0)"'!=0 or (tm or 0)!=0 or row.get('"'polishing_active')):
        add('frozen', key, 'inactive sites should not select operating infrastructure', row, 'zero modules and no polishing when inactive', 'set modules/tanks/polishing to zero/false or mark the site active')
for sid in sites:
    if seen_sites[sid]==0:
        add('schema', f'infrastructure/{sid}', 'one infrastructure row for every production site', 0, 1, 'add the missing site row')
    elif seen_sites[sid]>1:
        add('schema', f'infrastructure/{sid}', 'at most one infrastructure row per site', seen_sites[sid], 1, 'deduplicate the repeated site row')

infra_by_site={r['site_id']:r for r in infra if r.get('site_id') in sites}

# operations and shortfalls coverage
ops=sol['operations']
ops_map={}
for i,row in enumerate(ops):
    key=(row.get('scenario_id'), row.get('hour'), row.get('site_id'))
    label=f'operations[{i}]/{key}'
    for fld in ['scenario_id','hour','site_id','online_modules','startup_modules','production_kg','renewable_mwh','grid_mwh','storage_end_kg','vent_kg','water_m3','polishing_kg']:
        if fld not in row or row[fld] is None:
            add('schema', label, f'missing/non-null field {fld}', row.get(fld), 'present and non-null', 'populate the required field')
    if key in ops_map:
        add('schema', label, 'unique operations row per scenario-hour-site', key, 'unique key', 'remove the duplicate key row')
    ops_map[key]=row
    if row.get('scenario_id') not in scenarios:
        add('identifier', label, 'scenario_id must reference scenarios.csv', row.get('scenario_id'), 'known scenario', 'use a valid scenario_id')
    if row.get('site_id') not in sites:
        add('identifier', label, 'site_id must reference sites.csv', row.get('site_id'), 'known site', 'use a valid site_id')
    hour=row.get('hour')
    if not isinstance(hour,(int,float)) or not is_intlike(float(hour)):
        add('schema', label, 'hour must be a whole number', hour, 'integer 0..11', 'replace with an integer hour')
    else:
        hour=int(round(float(hour)))
        if hour < 0 or hour >= config['planning_horizon_hours']:
            add('time', label, 'hour must be within planning horizon', hour, f'0..{config[\"planning_horizon_hours\"]-1}', 'drop or correct the row hour')
    for fld in ['online_modules','startup_modules']:
        val=row.get(fld)
        if not isinstance(val,(int,float)) or not is_intlike(float(val)):
            add('schema', label, f'{fld} must be a whole number', val, 'integer', 'round to a valid integer consistent with state transitions')
    for fld in ['production_kg','renewable_mwh','grid_mwh','storage_end_kg','vent_kg','water_m3','polishing_kg']:

---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

description: Use when building, running, validating, or debugging Python optimization models that use PySCIPOpt and SCIP.
metadata:

---

# OR PySCIPOpt

## Use when

- The task asks for a Python optimization model using SCIP or `pyscipopt`.
- Existing solver code imports `pyscipopt`, sets SCIP parameters, writes `.lp`/`.mps`, or calls a SCIP-based checker.
- The user asks to run, debug, speed up, validate, or explain a SCIP-based solve.

## Do not use when

- The task only needs a mathematical formulation and no solver-specific code.
- The project clearly uses a different solver stack, such as Pyomo with CBC/HiGHS, OR-Tools CP-SAT, PuLP, CPLEX, or cvxpy, unless the user asks to port it to PySCIPOpt.
- The model is nonlinear or stochastic in a way that SCIP cannot handle directly; first decide whether it can be linearized or approximated.

## Environment rule

Use the Python environment specified by the user or the project. In this ORClaw workspace, the default solver environment is the `or` conda environment with PySCIPOpt available:

```bash
conda run -n or python <script.py>
```

If `pyscipopt` is missing, report the missing dependency, identify the command that failed, and try the project-specified environment before changing solver stacks.

## Workflow

1. Read the task brief and input files before coding.
2. Write the full mathematical model first: sets, parameters, decision variables, objective, and constraints.
3. Implement the model with explicit tuple keys and named constraints.
4. Solve with the selected Python command.
5. Export the solution using the exact schema required by the task.
6. Validate with the task checker or an independent recomputation of feasibility and objective.
7. If the solution is not accepted, diagnose whether the issue is formulation, data parsing, export formatting, tolerance, or evaluator mismatch before changing the objective or constraints.

## PySCIPOpt patterns

- Use `from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum`.
- Create sparse variable dictionaries explicitly, for example:

```python
x = {(i, j, t): model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"x[{i},{j},{t}]") for i, j, t in keys}
```

- Use `quicksum(...)` for large linear expressions.
- Use `model.addCons(expr <= rhs, name="capacity[k,t]")` for constraints.
- Use `model.setObjective(expr, "minimize")` or `model.setObjective(expr, "maximize")`.
- Set reproducibility and limits with SCIP parameters:

```python
model.setParam("limits/gap", 0.0005)
model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
```

- After `model.optimize()`, inspect `model.getStatus()` and only read values if a solution exists.
- Read variable values with `model.getVal(x[key])`, not `x[key].X`.
- Read objective and bound with `model.getObjVal()` and `model.getDualbound()`.
- Read relative gap with `model.getGap()` when available.

## Modeling hygiene

- Calibrate every big-M from data. If a tight M is not obvious, derive a bound and document it in a short comment.
- Keep feasibility checks, objective recomputation, and reference-optimality comparison separate.
- Do not round exported decision values unless the schema explicitly requires it.
- For benchmark work, treat the evaluator or checker as the scoring authority and the reference solver as a validation baseline.
- Keep solver logs or a concise solve summary when the user needs auditability.
- Avoid changing data to make a model feasible unless the task is explicitly to design or repair an instance.

## Debugging boundaries

- If a model is infeasible, inspect hard lower bounds, conservation equations, and mutually exclusive binary logic before relaxing constraints.
- If objective values differ, recompute each objective component from exported decisions.
- If runtime is high, first remove dominated variables, tighten big-M values, add eligibility filters, and check whether binaries are really necessary.
- If SCIP returns an incumbent with a gap, say so clearly; do not call it exact optimality unless the status and gap support that claim.
- If an evaluator rejects a solution that the model considers feasible, replay the rejected rule directly from the exported file before changing solver code.

## Useful commands

```bash
python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
conda run -n or python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
<python-command> <solver-script.py>
<python-command> <checker-script.py> --solution <solution-file> --env-dir <task-dir>
```

---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

# Agent Brief: Green Hydrogen Hub Resilience Planning

You are helping a regional clean-energy authority prepare a one-day operating
and resilience plan for a network of green hydrogen production hubs. The
network serves transit depots, critical backup-power customers, port drayage,
an industrial pilot, and airport ground equipment. Renewable power, demand,
water permits, module derates, and road capacity all vary across the supplied
stress scenarios.

# Green Hydrogen Hub Resilience Planning

## Planning Goal

Prepare a risk-aware plan for a regional green hydrogen hub over a 12-hour
operating day. The authority must decide a common infrastructure posture before
knowing which stress scenario occurs. After the scenario is realized, the plan
may adapt hourly production, renewable and grid energy use, storage, and tube
trailer deliveries.

The goal is to minimize daily infrastructure cost plus expected operating cost
and a tail-risk surcharge for expensive scenarios. Operating costs include
electrolyzer operation, grid energy, water, module starts, high-purity
polishing, trailer trips, emissions, venting, and unmet hydrogen demand.

## Operational Rules

Infrastructure decisions are shared across all scenarios. A site can operate
only within its selected electrolyzer-module count, tank-module count, and
polishing decision. Tank modules increase usable storage above the listed base
storage. High-purity customers can be served only by hydrogen that was polished
at a site with polishing capability.

Every scenario policy must account for each hour of production-site operation.
Online modules are integer counts. If modules are online, production must be at
least the site's minimum-load fraction and no more than the scenario-adjusted
module capacity. Module starts are limited over the day. Production consumes
energy and water. Renewable energy is limited by the scenario-adjusted
forecast; grid energy is limited by the scenario site-hour cap and counts
toward the scenario emissions cap.

Hydrogen storage is stateful at every site. Ending inventory equals prior
inventory plus production minus outbound deliveries and venting. Inventories
must stay within the selected storage capacity, and final inventory must meet
the site requirement. The whole network must also maintain a system reserve at
the end of every hour.

Deliveries move along listed site-customer lanes. Each lane has a tube-trailer
payload, trip cost, driver-hours requirement, normal hourly trip cap, and
hazmat delivery window. Scenario-hour rows may close or tighten a lane. A
delivery row cannot exceed the trip capacity implied by its submitted trip
count, and scenario-hour driver hours cannot exceed the scenario cap.

Every customer-hour demand must be either delivered or listed as shortfall.
Scenario-level service floors apply by customer. Critical customers have much
tighter hourly shortfall caps, so delaying them across several hours is risky
even if total daily service looks acceptable.

This task is designed for a short solve budget. A strong approach may combine
an infrastructure master problem, scenario subproblems, warm-started MIPs, and
repair heuristics for truck trips and inventories rather than relying on one
large cold-start model.

## Data Dictionary

`data/config.json`

- global conversion factors, reserve target, risk settings, penalties, and
  numerical tolerance.

`data/sites.csv`

- production sites, electrolyzer module limits, minimum load, variable costs,
  tank options, initial/final inventories, water permits, startup limits, and
  polishing costs.

`data/customers.csv`

- customer segments, purity requirement, service floor, hourly shortfall cap,
  and shortage penalty.

`data/lanes.csv`

- eligible tube-trailer lanes from production sites to customers, payloads,
  hourly trip caps, driver hours, trip cost, and hazmat delivery windows.

`data/scenarios.csv`

- scenario probability, narrative label, grid-price multiplier, emissions cap,
  and driver-hour budget.

`data/hourly_forecast.csv`

- site-hour renewable availability, grid price, and grid emissions intensity
  before scenario multipliers.

`data/base_customer_hourly_demand.csv`

- baseline customer demand profile used to interpret scenario demand shifts.

`data/scenario_site_hourly.csv`

- scenario-hour renewable, module-capacity, water-permit, and grid-capacity
  multipliers for each production site.

`data/scenario_customer_hourly_demand.csv`

- realized customer-hour hydrogen demand in each scenario.

`data/scenario_lane_hourly.csv`

- scenario-hour lane trip caps, travel multipliers, and closure flags.

## Submission Format

Create `submissions/solution.json`. The top-level `solution.json` object must contain:

```json
{
  "infrastructure": [],
  "operations": [],
  "deliveries": [],
  "shortfalls": []
}
```

Use one `infrastructure` row for every production site. Use one `operations`
row for every scenario, hour, and site. Use one `shortfalls` row for every
scenario, hour, and customer. `deliveries` may be sparse, but every delivery
row must reference a known lane and include both delivered kilograms and truck
trip count.

`infrastructure` rows:

```json
{
  "site_id": "PORT_SOLAR",
  "active": true,
  "electrolyzer_modules": 4,
  "tank_modules": 3,
```

`shortfalls` rows:

```json
{
  "scenario_id": "BASE",
  "hour": 1,
  "customer_id": "BUS_DEPOT",
  "kg": 1.0
}
```

Use nonnegative numeric values. Hours are `0` through `11`. Integer fields must
be submitted as whole numbers.

## Required Output

Create these artifacts under `submissions/`:

- `model.md`: a plain-language mathematical model or algorithm description.
- `solve.py`: the script used to create the submitted policy.
- `solution.json`: the infrastructure and scenario policy.
- `solve_log.md`: commands run, solver status, objective or score if known, and
  validation notes.

/bin/bash -lc "PYTHONPATH=/opt/or-deps python - <<'PY'
import csv, json
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path
base = Path('/app/data')
config = json.loads((base/'config.json').read_text())

# parse core tables
sites = list(csv.DictReader((base/'sites.csv').open()))
customers = list(csv.DictReader((base/'customers.csv').open()))
lanes = list(csv.DictReader((base/'lanes.csv').open()))
scenarios = list(csv.DictReader((base/'scenarios.csv').open()))
forecast = list(csv.DictReader((base/'hourly_forecast.csv').open()))
site_hour = list(csv.DictReader((base/'scenario_site_hourly.csv').open()))
demand = list(csv.DictReader((base/'scenario_customer_hourly_demand.csv').open()))
lane_hour = list(csv.DictReader((base/'scenario_lane_hourly.csv').open()))

site_ids = [r['site_id'] for r in sites]
scenario_ids = [r['scenario_id'] for r in scenarios]
customer_ids = [r['customer_id'] for r in customers]

print('Counts: sites', len(site_ids), 'customers', len(customer_ids), 'lanes', len(lanes), 'scenarios', len(scenarios), 'hours', config['planning_horizon_hours'])

# capacity and storage
print('\\nSite capacity / storage summary')
for s in sites:
    max_prod = float(s['max_modules']) * float(s['module_capacity_kgph'])
    max_store = float(s['tank_base_kg']) + float(s['max_tank_modules']) * float(s['tank_module_capacity_kg'])
    print(s['site_id'], 'max prod kg/h', max_prod, 'max storage', max_store, 'initial', s['initial_inventory_kg'], 'final min', s['final_min_inventory_kg'], 'water/day', s['water_limit_m3_per_day'], 'startups', s['startup_limit'])

print('\\nScenario total demand and service minima')
service_floor = {c['customer_id']: float(c['service_floor_fraction']) for c in customers}
for scen in scenario_ids:
    total = sum(float(r['demand_kg']) for r in demand if r['scenario_id']==scen)
    floor = sum(float(r['demand_kg']) * service_floor[r['customer_id']] for r in demand if r['scenario_id']==scen)
    critical_hour_cap = max(float(r['demand_kg']) * next(float(c['max_hourly_shortfall_fraction']) for c in customers if c['customer_id']==r['customer_id']) for r in demand if r['scenario_id']==scen)
    print(scen, 'total demand', round(total,2), 'floor total', round(floor,2), 'max allowed hourly shortfall any customer-hour', round(critical_hour_cap,2))

print('\\nPotential production upper bounds by scenario (ignoring startup/inventory/energy split)')
forecast_map = {(r['site_id'], int(r['hour'])): r for r in forecast}
site_hour_map = {(r['scenario_id'], r['site_id'], int(r['hour'])): r for r in site_hour}
for scen in scenario_ids:
    total_prod = 0.0
    total_ren = 0.0
    total_grid = 0.0
    for s in sites:
        sid = s['site_id']
        max_modules = int(s['max_modules'])
        cap = float(s['module_capacity_kgph'])
        for h in range(config['planning_horizon_hours']):
            sh = site_hour_map[(scen, sid, h)]
            f = forecast_map[(sid, h)]
            prod_cap = max_modules * cap * float(sh['module_capacity_multiplier'])
            ren_cap_prod = float(f['renewable_mwh']) * float(sh['renewable_multiplier']) / config['energy_mwh_per_kg_h2']
            grid_cap_prod = float(sh['grid_cap_mwh']) / config['energy_mwh_per_kg_h2']
            total_prod += min(prod_cap, ren_cap_prod + grid_cap_prod)
            total_ren += ren_cap_prod
            total_grid += grid_cap_prod
    print(scen, 'upper-bound producible kg', round(total_prod,1), 'ren-equiv kg', round(total_ren,1), 'grid-equiv kg', round(total_grid,1))

print('\\nLane and driver bottlenecks by scenario')
base_lane = {r['lane_id']: r for r in lanes}
for scen in scenario_ids:
    lane_caps = defaultdict(float)
    cust_caps = defaultdict(float)
    driver_use_nom = 0.0
    closed = 0
    for r in lane_hour:
        if r['scenario_id'] "'!= scen:
            continue
        lane = base_lane[r['"'lane_id']]
        trips = float(r['max_trips'])
        kg = trips * float(lane['kg_per_trip'])
        lane_caps[r['lane_id']] += kg
        cust_caps[lane['customer_id']] += kg
        driver_use_nom += trips * float(lane['driver_hours_per_trip']) * float(r['travel_time_multiplier'])
        closed += int(r['closed'])
    print(scen, 'closed rows', closed, 'aggregate lane kg cap', round(sum(lane_caps.values()),1), 'aggregate customer delivery kg cap', {k: round(v,1) for k,v in cust_caps.items()}, 'nominal all-lanes driver hours', round(driver_use_nom,1))

print('\\nCustomers requiring polishing assumption candidate (purity >= 99.9):', [c['customer_id'] for c in customers if float(c['purity_required_pct']) >= 99.9])
PY"

        caps={int(float(r['max_trips'])) for r in x}
        mults={float(r['travel_time_multiplier']) for r in x}
        closed=sum(int(r['closed']) for r in x)

            changed.append((lane, sorted(caps), sorted(mults), closed))

        print(item)
    print('"'changed lanes', len(changed))

#!/usr/bin/env python3

import copy
import csv
import json
import math
import os
import sys
import time
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum


ROOT = Path("/app")
DATA_DIR = ROOT / "data"
SUBMISSIONS_DIR = ROOT / "submissions"
SOLUTION_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solution.json"
LOG_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solve_log.md"
GAP_LIMIT = 0.0005
DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT = 300.0
HIGH_PURITY_THRESHOLD = 99.9


def load_json(path):
    with path.open() as handle:
        return json.load(handle)


def load_csv(path):
    with path.open(newline="") as handle:
        return list(csv.DictReader(handle))


def as_float(value):
    return float(value)


def as_int(value):
    return int(round(float(value)))


def clean_float(value, digits=8):
    if abs(value) < 1e-10:
        value = 0.0
    return float(f"{value:.{digits}f}")


def clean_int(value):
    return int(round(value))


def parse_time_limit():
    raw = os.environ.get("ORCLAW_SOLVE_TIME_LIMIT_SECONDS")
    if not raw:
        return DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT
    try:
        return max(1.0, min(DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT, float(raw)))
    except ValueError:
        return DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT


def build_data():
    config = load_json(DATA_DIR / "config.json")
    tol = as_float(config["numerical_tolerance"])
    hours = list(range(as_int(config["planning_horizon_hours"])))

    sites = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "sites.csv"):
        site_id = row["site_id"]
        sites[site_id] = {
            "site_id": site_id,
            "region": row["region"],
            "max_modules": as_int(row["max_modules"]),
            "module_capacity_kgph": as_float(row["module_capacity_kgph"]),
            "min_load_fraction": as_float(row["min_load_fraction"]),
            "module_daily_cost": as_float(row["module_daily_cost"]),
            "variable_cost_per_kg": as_float(row["variable_cost_per_kg"]),
            "tank_base_kg": as_float(row["tank_base_kg"]),
            "tank_module_capacity_kg": as_float(row["tank_module_capacity_kg"]),
            "max_tank_modules": as_int(row["max_tank_modules"]),
            "tank_daily_cost": as_float(row["tank_daily_cost"]),
            "initial_inventory_kg": as_float(row["initial_inventory_kg"]),
            "final_min_inventory_kg": as_float(row["final_min_inventory_kg"]),
            "water_limit_m3_per_day": as_float(row["water_limit_m3_per_day"]),
            "startup_limit": as_int(row["startup_limit"]),
            "polishing_cost_per_kg": as_float(row["polishing_cost_per_kg"]),
            "polishing_daily_cost": as_float(row["polishing_daily_cost"]),
        }

    customers = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "customers.csv"):
        customer_id = row["customer_id"]
        customers[customer_id] = {
            "customer_id": customer_id,
            "segment": row["segment"],
            "region": row["region"],
            "purity_required_pct": as_float(row["purity_required_pct"]),
            "service_floor_fraction": as_float(row["service_floor_fraction"]),
            "max_hourly_shortfall_fraction": as_float(row["max_hourly_shortfall_fraction"]),
            "shortfall_cost_per_kg": as_float(row["shortfall_cost_per_kg"]),
            "priority_weight": as_float(row["priority_weight"]),
        }

    lanes = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "lanes.csv"):
        lane_id = row["lane_id"]
        lanes[lane_id] = {
            "lane_id": lane_id,
            "origin_site": row["origin_site"],
            "customer_id": row["customer_id"],
            "distance_km": as_float(row["distance_km"]),
            "kg_per_trip": as_float(row["kg_per_trip"]),
            "base_max_trips_per_hour": as_int(row["base_max_trips_per_hour"]),
            "driver_hours_per_trip": as_float(row["driver_hours_per_trip"]),
            "cost_per_trip": as_float(row["cost_per_trip"]),
            "hazmat_window_start": as_int(row["hazmat_window_start"]),
            "hazmat_window_end": as_int(row["hazmat_window_end"]),
        }

    scenarios = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenarios.csv"):
        scenario_id = row["scenario_id"]
        scenarios[scenario_id] = {
            "scenario_id": scenario_id,
            "probability": as_float(row["probability"]),
            "description": row["description"],
            "grid_price_multiplier": as_float(row["grid_price_multiplier"]),
            "emission_cap_kg": as_float(row["emission_cap_kg"]),
            "driver_hours_cap": as_float(row["driver_hours_cap"]),
        }

    forecast = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "hourly_forecast.csv"):
        forecast[(row["site_id"], as_int(row["hour"]))] = {
            "renewable_mwh": as_float(row["renewable_mwh"]),
            "grid_price_per_mwh": as_float(row["grid_price_per_mwh"]),
            "grid_emission_kg_per_mwh": as_float(row["grid_emission_kg_per_mwh"]),
        }

    scenario_site = {}
    water_multiplier = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenario_site_hourly.csv"):
        key = (row["scenario_id"], row["site_id"], as_int(row["hour"]))
        scenario_site[key] = {
            "renewable_multiplier": as_float(row["renewable_multiplier"]),
            "module_capacity_multiplier": as_float(row["module_capacity_multiplier"]),
            "water_limit_multiplier": as_float(row["water_limit_multiplier"]),
            "grid_cap_mwh": as_float(row["grid_cap_mwh"]),
        }
        ws_key = (row["scenario_id"], row["site_id"])
        value = scenario_site[key]["water_limit_multiplier"]
        prior = water_multiplier.get(ws_key)
        if prior is None:
            water_multiplier[ws_key] = value
        elif abs(prior - value) > tol:
            raise ValueError(f"Water multiplier varies within scenario-site {ws_key}")

    scenario_demand = {}
    daily_demand = defaultdict(float)
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenario_customer_hourly_demand.csv"):
        key = (row["scenario_id"], row["customer_id"], as_int(row["hour"]))
        demand = as_float(row["demand_kg"])
        scenario_demand[key] = demand
        daily_demand[(row["scenario_id"], row["customer_id"])] += demand

    scenario_lane = {}
    for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenario_lane_hourly.csv"):
        scenario_lane[(row["scenario_id"], row["lane_id"], as_int(row["hour"]))] = {
            "max_trips": as_int(row["max_trips"]),
            "travel_time_multiplier": as_float(row["travel_time_multiplier"]),
            "closed": as_int(row["closed"]),
        }

    base_demand = {
        (row["customer_id"], as_int(row["hour"])): as_float(row["demand_kg"])
        for row in load_csv(DATA_DIR / "base_customer_hourly_demand.csv")
    }

    site_ids = sorted(sites)
    customer_ids = sorted(customers)
    lane_ids = sorted(lanes)
    scenario_ids = sorted(scenarios)

    probability_sum = sum(scenarios[w]["probability"] for w in scenario_ids)
    if abs(probability_sum - 1.0) > 1e-8:
        raise ValueError(f"Scenario probabilities sum to {probability_sum}, not 1")

    high_purity_customers = {
        c for c in customer_ids if customers[c]["purity_required_pct"] >= HIGH_PURITY_THRESHOLD
    }

    lanes_by_site = defaultdict(list)
    lanes_by_customer = defaultdict(list)
    lanes_by_site_high = defaultdict(list)
    lanes_by_site_low = defaultdict(list)
    for lane_id, lane in lanes.items():
        lanes_by_site[lane["origin_site"]].append(lane_id)
        lanes_by_customer[lane["customer_id"]].append(lane_id)
        if lane["customer_id"] in high_purity_customers:
            lanes_by_site_high[lane["origin_site"]].append(lane_id)
        else:
            lanes_by_site_low[lane["origin_site"]].append(lane_id)

    max_polish = {}
    for site_id, site in sites.items():
        max_polish[site_id] = site["initial_inventory_kg"] + len(hours) * site["max_modules"] * site["module_capacity_kgph"]

    return {
        "config": config,
        "tol": tol,
        "hours": hours,
        "sites": sites,
        "customers": customers,
        "lanes": lanes,
        "scenarios": scenarios,
        "forecast": forecast,
        "scenario_site": scenario_site,
        "scenario_demand": scenario_demand,
        "scenario_lane": scenario_lane,
        "base_demand": base_demand,
        "daily_demand": dict(daily_demand),
        "site_ids": site_ids,
        "customer_ids": customer_ids,
        "lane_ids": lane_ids,
        "scenario_ids": scenario_ids,
        "high_purity_customers": high_purity_customers,
        "lanes_by_site": dict(lanes_by_site),
        "lanes_by_customer": dict(lanes_by_customer),
        "lanes_by_site_high": dict(lanes_by_site_high),
        "lanes_by_site_low": dict(lanes_by_site_low),
        "water_multiplier": water_multiplier,
        "max_polish": max_polish,
    }


def build_model(data, time_limit):
    config = data["config"]
    hours = data["hours"]
    site_ids = data["site_ids"]
    lane_ids = data["lane_ids"]
    customer_ids = data["customer_ids"]
    scenario_ids = data["scenario_ids"]
    sites = data["sites"]
    lanes = data["lanes"]
    customers = data["customers"]
    scenarios = data["scenarios"]
    forecast = data["forecast"]
    scenario_site = data["scenario_site"]
    scenario_demand = data["scenario_demand"]
    scenario_lane = data["scenario_lane"]

    model = Model("green_hydrogen_hub_resilience")
    model.setParam("limits/gap", GAP_LIMIT)
    model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
    model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)

    active = {}
    modules = {}
    tank_modules = {}
    polishing_active = {}
    for site_id in site_ids:
        site = sites[site_id]
        active[site_id] = model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"active[{site_id}]")
        modules[site_id] = model.addVar(vtype="I", lb=0, ub=site["max_modules"], name=f"modules[{site_id}]")
        tank_modules[site_id] = model.addVar(vtype="I", lb=0, ub=site["max_tank_modules"], name=f"tank[{site_id}]")
        polishing_active[site_id] = model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"polishing[{site_id}]")

    online = {}
    startup = {}
    production = {}
    renewable = {}
    grid = {}
    polished = {}
    unpolished_inventory = {}
    polished_inventory = {}
    vent_unpolished = {}
    vent_polished = {}
    trips = {}
    delivered = {}
    shortfall = {}
    excess = {}

    for scenario_id in scenario_ids:
        for site_id in site_ids:
            site = sites[site_id]
            for hour in hours:
                key = (scenario_id, site_id, hour)
                online[key] = model.addVar(vtype="I", lb=0, ub=site["max_modules"], name=f"on[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                startup[key] = model.addVar(vtype="I", lb=0, ub=site["max_modules"], name=f"start[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                production[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"prod[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                renewable[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"ren[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                grid[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"grid[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                polished[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"pol[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                unpolished_inventory[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"uinv[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                polished_inventory[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"pinv[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                vent_unpolished[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"ventu[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                vent_polished[key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"ventp[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
        for lane_id in lane_ids:
            lane = lanes[lane_id]
            for hour in hours:
                lane_key = (scenario_id, lane_id, hour)
                cap = scenario_lane[lane_key]["max_trips"]
                trips[lane_key] = model.addVar(vtype="I", lb=0, ub=cap, name=f"trip[{scenario_id},{lane_id},{hour}]")
                delivered[lane_key] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, ub=lane["kg_per_trip"] * cap, name=f"del[{scenario_id},{lane_id},{hour}]")
        for customer_id in customer_ids:
            for hour in hours:
                shortfall[(scenario_id, customer_id, hour)] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"short[{scenario_id},{customer_id},{hour}]")
        excess[scenario_id] = model.addVar(lb=0.0, name=f"excess[{scenario_id}]")

    eta = model.addVar(lb=-model.infinity(), name="eta")

    for site_id in site_ids:
        site = sites[site_id]
        model.addCons(modules[site_id] <= site["max_modules"] * active[site_id], name=f"module_select[{site_id}]")
        model.addCons(
            tank_modules[site_id] <= site["max_tank_modules"] * active[site_id],
            name=f"tank_select[{site_id}]",
        )
tool65. Command (exit 0)
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '321,640p' /app/submissions/solve.py"
result66. Command output
        model.addCons(polishing_active[site_id] <= active[site_id], name=f"polish_select[{site_id}]")

    operating_cost_expr = {}
    for scenario_id in scenario_ids:
        scenario = scenarios[scenario_id]
        for site_id in site_ids:
            site = sites[site_id]
            model.addCons(
                quicksum(startup[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)] for hour in hours) <= site["startup_limit"],
                name=f"startup_limit[{scenario_id},{site_id}]",
            )
            model.addCons(
                quicksum(config["water_m3_per_kg_h2"] * production[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)] for hour in hours)
                <= site["water_limit_m3_per_day"] * data["water_multiplier"][(scenario_id, site_id)],
                name=f"water[{scenario_id},{site_id}]",
            )
            for hour in hours:
                key = (scenario_id, site_id, hour)
                sh = scenario_site[key]
                fc = forecast[(site_id, hour)]
                low_delivery = quicksum(
                    delivered[(scenario_id, lane_id, hour)] for lane_id in data["lanes_by_site_low"].get(site_id, [])
                )
                high_delivery = quicksum(
                    delivered[(scenario_id, lane_id, hour)] for lane_id in data["lanes_by_site_high"].get(site_id, [])
                )

                model.addCons(online[key] <= modules[site_id], name=f"online[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                if hour == 0:
                    model.addCons(startup[key] >= online[key], name=f"startup0[{scenario_id},{site_id}]")
                else:
                    prev = (scenario_id, site_id, hour - 1)
                    model.addCons(startup[key] >= online[key] - online[prev], name=f"startup[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")

                cap = site["module_capacity_kgph"] * sh["module_capacity_multiplier"]
                model.addCons(production[key] <= cap * online[key], name=f"produb[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                model.addCons(
                    production[key] >= site["min_load_fraction"] * cap * online[key],
                    name=f"prodlb[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                )
                model.addCons(
                    config["energy_mwh_per_kg_h2"] * production[key]
                    + config["polishing_mwh_per_kg"] * polished[key]
                    <= renewable[key] + grid[key],
                    name=f"energy[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                )
                model.addCons(
                    renewable[key] <= fc["renewable_mwh"] * sh["renewable_multiplier"],
                    name=f"rencap[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                )
                model.addCons(grid[key] <= sh["grid_cap_mwh"], name=f"gridcap[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]")
                model.addCons(
                    polished[key] <= data["max_polish"][site_id] * polishing_active[site_id],
                    name=f"polcap[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                )

                if hour == 0:
                    model.addCons(
                        unpolished_inventory[key]
                        == site["initial_inventory_kg"] + production[key] - polished[key] - low_delivery - vent_unpolished[key],
                        name=f"uinv0[{scenario_id},{site_id}]",
                    )
                    model.addCons(
                        polished_inventory[key] == polished[key] - high_delivery - vent_polished[key],
                        name=f"pinv0[{scenario_id},{site_id}]",
                    )
                else:
                    prev = (scenario_id, site_id, hour - 1)
                    model.addCons(
                        unpolished_inventory[key]
                        == unpolished_inventory[prev] + production[key] - polished[key] - low_delivery - vent_unpolished[key],
                        name=f"uinv[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                    )
                    model.addCons(
                        polished_inventory[key]
                        == polished_inventory[prev] + polished[key] - high_delivery - vent_polished[key],
                        name=f"pinv[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                    )

                model.addCons(
                    unpolished_inventory[key] + polished_inventory[key]
                    <= site["tank_base_kg"] + site["tank_module_capacity_kg"] * tank_modules[site_id],
                    name=f"storage[{scenario_id},{site_id},{hour}]",
                )

            final_key = (scenario_id, site_id, hours[-1])
            model.addCons(
                unpolished_inventory[final_key] + polished_inventory[final_key] >= site["final_min_inventory_kg"],
                name=f"final[{scenario_id},{site_id}]",
            )

        for lane_id in lane_ids:
            lane = lanes[lane_id]
            origin = lane["origin_site"]
            for hour in hours:
                lane_key = (scenario_id, lane_id, hour)
                model.addCons(
                    trips[lane_key] <= scenario_lane[lane_key]["max_trips"] * active[origin],
                    name=f"tripactive[{scenario_id},{lane_id},{hour}]",
                )
                model.addCons(
                    delivered[lane_key] <= lane["kg_per_trip"] * trips[lane_key],
                    name=f"payload[{scenario_id},{lane_id},{hour}]",
                )

        for hour in hours:
            model.addCons(
                quicksum(
                    lanes[lane_id]["driver_hours_per_trip"]
                    * scenario_lane[(scenario_id, lane_id, hour)]["travel_time_multiplier"]
                    * trips[(scenario_id, lane_id, hour)]
                    for lane_id in lane_ids
                )
                <= scenario["driver_hours_cap"],
                name=f"drivers[{scenario_id},{hour}]",
            )
            model.addCons(
                quicksum(
                    unpolished_inventory[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)] + polished_inventory[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                    for site_id in site_ids
                )
                >= config["system_min_reserve_kg"],
                name=f"reserve[{scenario_id},{hour}]",
            )

        for customer_id in customer_ids:
            customer = customers[customer_id]
            model.addCons(
                quicksum(shortfall[(scenario_id, customer_id, hour)] for hour in hours)
                <= (1.0 - customer["service_floor_fraction"]) * data["daily_demand"][(scenario_id, customer_id)],
                name=f"service[{scenario_id},{customer_id}]",
            )
            for hour in hours:
                demand = scenario_demand[(scenario_id, customer_id, hour)]
                model.addCons(
                    quicksum(delivered[(scenario_id, lane_id, hour)] for lane_id in data["lanes_by_customer"][customer_id])
                    + shortfall[(scenario_id, customer_id, hour)]
                    == demand,
                    name=f"demand[{scenario_id},{customer_id},{hour}]",
                )
                model.addCons(
                    shortfall[(scenario_id, customer_id, hour)]
                    <= customer["max_hourly_shortfall_fraction"] * demand,
                    name=f"shortcap[{scenario_id},{customer_id},{hour}]",
                )

        model.addCons(
            quicksum(
                forecast[(site_id, hour)]["grid_emission_kg_per_mwh"] * grid[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                for site_id in site_ids
                for hour in hours
            )
            <= scenario["emission_cap_kg"],
            name=f"emissions[{scenario_id}]",
        )

        operating_cost_expr[scenario_id] = (
            quicksum(
                sites[site_id]["variable_cost_per_kg"] * production[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                + config["water_cost_per_m3"] * config["water_m3_per_kg_h2"] * production[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                + config["startup_cost_per_module"] * startup[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                + sites[site_id]["polishing_cost_per_kg"] * polished[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                + config["vent_penalty_per_kg"]
                * (vent_unpolished[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)] + vent_polished[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)])
                + scenario["grid_price_multiplier"]
                * forecast[(site_id, hour)]["grid_price_per_mwh"]
                * grid[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                + config["emission_cost_per_kg"]
                * forecast[(site_id, hour)]["grid_emission_kg_per_mwh"]
                * grid[(scenario_id, site_id, hour)]
                for site_id in site_ids
                for hour in hours
            )
            + quicksum(
                lanes[lane_id]["cost_per_trip"] * trips[(scenario_id, lane_id, hour)]
                for lane_id in lane_ids
                for hour in hours
            )
            + quicksum(
                customers[customer_id]["shortfall_cost_per_kg"]
                * customers[customer_id]["priority_weight"]
                * shortfall[(scenario_id, customer_id, hour)]
                for customer_id in customer_ids
                for hour in hours
            )
        )
        model.addCons(excess[scenario_id] >= operating_cost_expr[scenario_id] - eta, name=f"cvar[{scenario_id}]")

    infra_cost = quicksum(
        sites[site_id]["module_daily_cost"] * modules[site_id]
        + sites[site_id]["tank_daily_cost"] * tank_modules[site_id]
        + sites[site_id]["polishing_daily_cost"] * polishing_active[site_id]
        for site_id in site_ids
    )
    expected_cost = quicksum(
        scenarios[scenario_id]["probability"] * operating_cost_expr[scenario_id] for scenario_id in scenario_ids
    )
    cvar_cost = eta + (1.0 / (1.0 - config["tail_risk_alpha"])) * quicksum(
        scenarios[scenario_id]["probability"] * excess[scenario_id] for scenario_id in scenario_ids
    )
    model.setObjective(infra_cost + expected_cost + config["tail_risk_weight"] * cvar_cost, "minimize")

    return model, {
        "active": active,
        "modules": modules,
        "tank_modules": tank_modules,
        "polishing_active": polishing_active,
        "online": online,
        "startup": startup,
        "production": production,
        "renewable": renewable,
        "grid": grid,
        "polished": polished,
        "unpolished_inventory": unpolished_inventory,
        "polished_inventory": polished_inventory,
        "vent_unpolished": vent_unpolished,
        "vent_polished": vent_polished,
        "trips": trips,
        "delivered": delivered,
        "shortfall": shortfall,
    }

    44	def clean_float(value, digits=8):
    45	    if abs(value) < 1e-10:
    46	        value = 0.0
    47	    return float(f"{value:.{digits}f}")

    54	def parse_time_limit():
    55	    raw = os.environ.get("ORCLAW_SOLVE_TIME_LIMIT_SECONDS")
    56	    if not raw:
    57	        return DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT
    58	    try:
    59	        return max(1.0, min(DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT, float(raw)))
    60	    except ValueError:
    61	        return DEFAULT_TIME_LIMIT

   122	    scenarios = {}

   152	        ws_key = (row["scenario_id"], row["site_id"])
   153	        value = scenario_site[key]["water_limit_multiplier"]
   154	        prior = water_multiplier.get(ws_key)
   155	        if prior is None:
   156	            water_multiplier[ws_key] = value

   160	    scenario_demand = {}

   181	    site_ids = sorted(sites)

   186	    probability_sum = sum(scenarios[w]["probability"] for w in scenario_ids)

   190	    high_purity_customers = {

   194	    lanes_by_site = defaultdict(list)

   198	    for lane_id, lane in lanes.items():
   199	        lanes_by_site[lane["origin_site"]].append(lane_id)
   200	        lanes_by_customer[lane["customer_id"]].append(lane_id)
   201	        if lane["customer_id"] in high_purity_customers:
   202	            lanes_by_site_high[lane["origin_site"]].append(lane_id)
   203	        else:
   204	            lanes_by_site_low[lane["origin_site"]].append(lane_id)
   205
   206	    max_polish = {}
   207	    for site_id, site in sites.items():
   208	        max_polish[site_id] = site["initial_inventory_kg"] + len(hours) * site["max_modules"] * site["module_capacity_kgph"]
   209
   210	    return {

   238	def build_model(data, time_limit):
   239	    config = data["config"]
   240	    hours = data["hours"]
